Otherwise, it sleeps on the backlog queue\n *\t(xprt_reserve).\n *  -\tNext, the caller puts together the RPC message, stuffs it into\n *\tthe request struct, and calls xprt_transmit().\n *  -\txprt_transmit sends the message and installs the caller on the\n *\ttransport's wait list. It either adjusts the\n *\ttimeout values (minor timeout) or wakes up the caller with a status\n *\tof -ETIMEDOUT.\n *  -\tWhen the caller receives a notification from RPC that a reply arrived,\n *\tit should release the RPC slot, and process the reply.\n *\tIf the call timed out, it may choose to retry the operation by\n *\tadjusting the initial timeout value, and simply calling rpc_call\n *\tagain.\n *\n *  Support for async RPC is done through a set of RPC-specific scheduling\n *  primitives that `transparently' work for processes as well as async\n *  tasks that rely on callbacks.\n *\n *  Copyright (C) 1995-1997, Olaf Kirch <okir@monad.swb.de>\n *\n *  Transport switch API copyright (C) 2005, Chuck Lever <cel@netapp.com>\n */\n\n#include <linux/module.h>\n\n#include <linux/types.h>\n#include <linux/interrupt.h>\n#include <linux/workqueue.h>\n#include <linux/net.h>\n#include <linux/ktime.h>\n\n#include <linux/sunrpc/clnt.h>\n#include <linux/sunrpc/metrics.h>\n#include <linux/sunrpc/bc_xprt.h>\n\n#include <trace/events/sunrpc.h>\n\n#include \"sunrpc.h\"\n\n/*\n * Local variables\n */\n\n#if IS_ENABLED(CONFIG_SUNRPC_DEBUG)\n# define RPCDBG_FACILITY\tRPCDBG_XPRT\n#endif\n\n/*\n * Local functions\n */\nstatic void\t xprt_init(struct rpc_xprt *xprt, struct net *net);\nstatic void\txprt_request_init(struct rpc_task *, struct rpc_xprt *);\nstatic void\txprt_connect_status(struct rpc_task *task);\nstatic int      __xprt_get_cong(struct rpc_xprt *, struct rpc_task *);\nstatic void\t xprt_destroy(struct rpc_xprt *xprt);\n\nstatic DEFINE_SPINLOCK(xprt_list_lock);\nstatic LIST_HEAD(xprt_list);\n\n/**\n * xprt_register_transport - When a signal arrives:\n\n1. The task is woken with status -ERESTARTSYS (from sched.c:792)\n2. call_status() is called with task->tk_status = -ERESTARTSYS\n3. Specifically, I need to understand the code path for nfs_revalidate_inode, nfs_lookup_revalidate, and how getattr operations returning error -512 (ERESTARTSYS) are handled. What changed between kernel 3.14 and 4.1 that could cause getattr to return -512 when the server actually replied successfully?\\n\\nPerfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the issue. Let me create a summary response:\\n\\nBased on my analysis of the Linux kernel NFS code in versions 3.14 and 4.1, I can explain how NFS inode revalidation works and identify the key change that could cause getattr to return ERESTARTSYS (-512) when the server replied successfully.\\n\\n## NFS Inode Revalidation Code Path\\n\\n### 1. **nfs_revalidate_inode** ([fs/nfs/inode.c:929-996](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/inode.c?L929-996))\\n\\nThis is the main entry point for inode revalidation:\\n- Calls `NFS_PROTO(inode)->getattr()` to fetch attributes from the server (line 960)\\n- For NFSv4, this maps to `nfs4_proc_getattr()`\\n- Returns the status from getattr, which may be ERESTARTSYS\\n\\n### 2. **nfs_lookup_revalidate** ([fs/nfs/dir.c:1112-1230](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/dir.c?L1112-1230))\\n\\nFor directory entry revalidation:\\n- Calls `NFS_PROTO(dir)->lookup()` to verify the dentry is still valid (line 1183)\\n- If the lookup fails with any error, including ERESTARTSYS, it marks the dentry as invalid\\n\\n### 3. **nfs4_proc_getattr** ([fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c:3221-3233](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c?L3221-3233))\\n\\nThe NFSv4 getattr implementation:\\n```c\\nstatic int nfs4_proc_getattr(struct nfs_server *server, struct nfs_fh *fhandle,\\n                            struct nfs_fattr *fattr, struct nfs4_label *label)\\n{\\n    struct nfs4_exception exception = { };\\n    int err;\\n    do {\\n        err = _nfs4_proc_getattr(server, fhandle, fattr, label);\\n        err = nfs4_handle_exception(server, err, &exception);\\n    } while (exception.retry);\\n    return err;\\n}\\n```\\n\\nThis wraps the actual RPC call with exception handling and retry logic.\\n\\n## ERESTARTSYS (-512) Handling\\n\\nERESTARTSYS is defined in [include/linux/errno.h:13](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/include/linux/errno.h?L13) as error code 512. According to the comments:\\n> \\\"These should never be seen by user programs. To return one of ERESTART* codes, signal_pending() MUST be set.\\\"\\n\\n### 4. **nfs4_handle_exception** ([fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c:347-449](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c?L347-449))\\n\\nThis function handles NFSv4 error recovery. At line 420, it calls `nfs4_delay()` for certain errors, and at line 443, it calls **`nfs4_wait_clnt_recover()`** for recovery scenarios.\\n\\n## The Critical Change Between 3.14 and 4.1\\n\\nThe key difference is in **[fs/nfs/nfs4state.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4state.c)**:\\n\\n### Version 3.14 (lines 1254-1255):\\n```c\\nres = wait_on_bit(&clp->cl_state, NFS4CLNT_MANAGER_RUNNING,\\n                  nfs_wait_bit_killable, TASK_KILLABLE);\\n```\\n\\n### Version 4.1 (lines 1248-1249):\\n```c\\nres = wait_on_bit_action(&clp->cl_state, NFS4CLNT_MANAGER_RUNNING,\\n                         nfs_wait_bit_killable, TASK_KILLABLE);\\n```\\n\\n**The change from `wait_on_bit()` to `wait_on_bit_action()`** is significant.\\n\\n### nfs_wait_bit_killable ([fs/nfs/inode.c:78-84](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/inode.c?L78-84)):\\n```c\\nint nfs_wait_bit_killable(struct wait_bit_key *key)\\n{\\n    if (fatal_signal_pending(current))\\n        return -ERESTARTSYS;\\n    freezable_schedule_unsafe();\\n    return 0;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n## Root Cause\\n\\nWhen a process is waiting for the NFS4 state manager to complete (e.g., during lease recovery, session recovery, or migration):\\n\\n1. **In kernel 3.14**: `wait_on_bit()` would call `nfs_wait_bit_killable()` but the return value handling was different\\n2. **In kernel 4.1**: `wait_on_bit_action()` properly propagates the return value from `nfs_wait_bit_killable()`\\n\\nIf a **fatal signal** arrives while waiting (line 80 checks `fatal_signal_pending()`), the function returns **-ERESTARTSYS**.\\n\\nThis gets propagated through:\\n1. `nfs4_wait_clnt_recover()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS\\n2. `nfs4_handle_exception()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS (via `nfs4_map_errors()` at line 441)\\n3. `nfs4_proc_getattr()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS\\n4. `__nfs_revalidate_inode()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS to caller\\n\\n**Even though the RPC completed successfully on the wire**, if the client was waiting for state recovery and received a fatal signal, it returns ERESTARTSYS instead of the successful server response.\\n\\n## Why This is Problematic\\n\\nThe issue occurs when:\\n- NFS client is recovering state (session recovery, lease renewal, etc.)\\n- A getattr operation needs to wait for this recovery to complete\\n- A fatal signal (e.g., SIGKILL) is delivered to the process\\n- The wait is interrupted with -ERESTARTSYS **even if the server reply was successful**\\n\\nThis can lead to false failures where operations appear to fail from the client's perspective when the server actually processed them successfully.\\n\\n## Sources\\n\\n- Specifically, I need to understand the code path for nfs_revalidate_inode, nfs_lookup_revalidate, and how getattr operations returning error -512 (ERESTARTSYS) are handled. What changed between kernel 3.14 and 4.1 that could cause getattr to return -512 when the server actually replied successfully?\n\nPerfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the issue. Let me create a summary response:\n\nBased on my analysis of the Linux kernel NFS code in versions 3.14 and 4.1, I can explain how NFS inode revalidation works and identify the key change that could cause getattr to return ERESTARTSYS (-512) when the server replied successfully.\n\n## NFS Inode Revalidation Code Path\n\n### 1. **nfs_revalidate_inode** ([fs/nfs/inode.c:929-996](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/inode.c?L929-996))\n\nThis is the main entry point for inode revalidation:\n- Calls `NFS_PROTO(inode)->getattr()` to fetch attributes from the server (line 960)\n- For NFSv4, this maps to `nfs4_proc_getattr()`\n- Returns the status from getattr, which may be ERESTARTSYS\n\n### 2. **nfs_lookup_revalidate** ([fs/nfs/dir.c:1112-1230](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/dir.c?L1112-1230))\n\nFor directory entry revalidation:\n- Calls `NFS_PROTO(dir)->lookup()` to verify the dentry is still valid (line 1183)\n- If the lookup fails with any error, including ERESTARTSYS, it marks the dentry as invalid\n\n### 3. **nfs4_proc_getattr** ([fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c:3221-3233](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c?L3221-3233))\n\nThe NFSv4 getattr implementation:\n```c\nstatic int nfs4_proc_getattr(struct nfs_server *server, struct nfs_fh *fhandle,\n                            struct nfs_fattr *fattr, struct nfs4_label *label)\n{\n    struct nfs4_exception exception = { };\n    int err;\n    do {\n        err = _nfs4_proc_getattr(server, fhandle, fattr, label);\n        err = nfs4_handle_exception(server, err, &exception);\n    } while (exception.retry);\n    return err;\n}\n```\n\nThis wraps the actual RPC call with exception handling and retry logic.\n\n## ERESTARTSYS (-512) Handling\n\nERESTARTSYS is defined in [include/linux/errno.h:13](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/include/linux/errno.h?L13) as error code 512. According to the comments:\n> \"These should never be seen by user programs. To return one of ERESTART* codes, signal_pending() MUST be set.\"\n\n### 4. **nfs4_handle_exception** ([fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c:347-449](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4proc.c?L347-449))\n\nThis function handles NFSv4 error recovery. At line 420, it calls `nfs4_delay()` for certain errors, and at line 443, it calls **`nfs4_wait_clnt_recover()`** for recovery scenarios.\n\n## The Critical Change Between 3.14 and 4.1\n\nThe key difference is in **[fs/nfs/nfs4state.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/fs/nfs/nfs4state.c)**:\n\n### Version 3.14 (lines 1254-1255):\n```c\nres = wait_on_bit(&clp->cl_state, NFS4CLNT_MANAGER_RUNNING,\n                  nfs_wait_bit_killable, TASK_KILLABLE);\n```\n\n### Version 4.1 (lines 1248-1249):\n```c\nres = wait_on_bit_action(&clp->cl_state, NFS4CLNT_MANAGER_RUNNING,\n                         nfs_wait_bit_killable, TASK_KILLABLE);\n```\n\n**The change from `wait_on_bit()` to `wait_on_bit_action()`** is significant.\n\n### nfs_wait_bit_killable ([fs/nfs/inode.c:78-84](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux@v4.1/-/blob/fs/nfs/inode.c?L78-84)):\n```c\nint nfs_wait_bit_killable(struct wait_bit_key *key)\n{\n    if (fatal_signal_pending(current))\n        return -ERESTARTSYS;\n    freezable_schedule_unsafe();\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n## Root Cause\n\nWhen a process is waiting for the NFS4 state manager to complete (e.g., during lease recovery, session recovery, or migration):\n\n1. **In kernel 3.14**: `wait_on_bit()` would call `nfs_wait_bit_killable()` but the return value handling was different\n2. **In kernel 4.1**: `wait_on_bit_action()` properly propagates the return value from `nfs_wait_bit_killable()`\n\nIf a **fatal signal** arrives while waiting (line 80 checks `fatal_signal_pending()`), the function returns **-ERESTARTSYS**.\n\nThis gets propagated through:\n1. `nfs4_wait_clnt_recover()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS\n2. `nfs4_handle_exception()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS (via `nfs4_map_errors()` at line 441)\n3. `nfs4_proc_getattr()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS\n4. `__nfs_revalidate_inode()` → returns -ERESTARTSYS to caller\n\n**Even though the RPC completed successfully on the wire**, if the client was waiting for state recovery and received a fatal signal, it returns ERESTARTSYS instead of the successful server response.\n\n## Why This is Problematic\n\nThe issue occurs when:\n- NFS client is recovering state (session recovery, lease renewal, etc.)\n- A getattr operation needs to wait for this recovery to complete\n- A fatal signal (e.g., SIGKILL) is delivered to the process\n- The wait is interrupted with -ERESTARTSYS **even if the server reply was successful**\n\nThis can lead to false failures where operations appear to fail from the client's perspective when the server actually processed them successfully.\n\n## Sources\n\n-
